<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the local <i>p</i>-adic section conjecture for smooth proper geometrically connected curves of genus at least two, over every finite extension of ℚ<sub><i>p</i></sub>. Combined with established finite-descent theorems, this also yields the global section conjecture for smooth proper geometrically connected curves of genus at least two over number fields when their finite-cover descent locus equals their rational points; this includes $`X_0(N)`$ and $`X_1(N)`$ of genus at least two over ℚ.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-section)): For every prime $p$, every finite extension $k/\mathbb Q_p$, and every smooth proper geometrically connected curve $X/k$ of genus at least two, the map $$X(k)\longrightarrow \left\{\begin{gathered} s:G_k\to\pi_1(X)\text{ a continuous group homomorphism}\\ \text{such that }(\pi_1(X)\to G_k)\circ s=\mathrm{id}_{G_k} \end{gathered}\right\}\big/\pi_1(X_{\bar k})$$ is bijective. The quotient is by conjugation by the full geometric étale fundamental group.
  - Explanation: Rational points correspond bijectively to full arithmetic étale section classes over finite extensions of the p-adic field.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-completion)): Let $p$ be a prime, let $k/\mathbb Q_p$ be finite, and let $X/k$ be a smooth proper geometrically connected curve of genus at least two. Put $K=k(X)$, choose a separable closure $K^{\mathrm{sep}}$, and let $\widetilde K\subset K^{\mathrm{sep}}$ be the compositum of the function fields of all connected finite étale covers of $X$, with all their $K$-embeddings. For every real-valued rank-one norm on $K$ extending the normalized $p$-adic norm on $k$, and every prolongation to $K^{\mathrm{sep}}$, set $$E=\widehat{K^{\mathrm{sep}}},\qquad H=\widehat{\widetilde K}\subset E,$$ where the completions use that fixed prolongation. Then the specified subfield $K^{\mathrm{sep}}$ of $E$ is contained in $H$.
  - Explanation: The specified separable closure lies in the completion of the universal étale function field for the fixed prolonged norm.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-local-normal-form)): Let $f:C\to X_{\bar k}$ be a finite surjective morphism of smooth proper connected curves, and let $b\in X(\bar k)$. Fix a rational function $t_b$ which is a local parameter at $b$. There is an open disk $B_b$ about $b$ in $X_\Omega^{\mathrm{an}}$, with coordinate $t_b$, such that its full inverse image in $C_\Omega^{\mathrm{an}}$ is a disjoint union of open disks. On the disk about $c\in f^{-1}(b)$ there is a coordinate $z_c$ for which $$t_b=z_c^{e_c},$$ where $e_c$ is the ramification index at $c$. No condition that $e_c$ be prime to $p$ is required. For finitely many distinct points $b$, the disks can be chosen disjoint and disjoint from the fixed point $\xi$.
  - Explanation: Branch disks admit power-map coordinates even when the ramification index is divisible by the residue prime.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-patching)): Let $f:C\to X_{\bar k}$ be as in Lemma <a href="#lem:local-normal-form" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:local-normal-form">3</a>, of degree $d$, with branch set $B$. Choose pairwise disjoint disks $B_b$ as in that lemma. For each $b\in B$, let $m_b$ be a positive multiple of all ramification indices above $b$. Suppose that $g:Y\to X_\Omega$ is a finite surjective morphism from a smooth proper connected curve and that there are radii $0<r_b<R_b$ in $\|\Omega^\times\|$, strictly inside $B_b$, such that $g^*t_b$ has an analytic $m_b$-th root on the whole inverse image of $$A_b=\{r_b<\|t_b\|<R_b\}.$$ Set $$U=X_\Omega^{\mathrm{an}}\setminus \bigcup_{b\in B}\{\|t_b\|\le r_b\},$$ where each indicated closed disk is taken inside $B_b$. Then there is a smooth proper connected curve $V_\Omega$ and a finite étale surjective map $V_\Omega\to Y$, together with an analytic map $$(V_\Omega^{\mathrm{an}})\|_U\longrightarrow C_\Omega^{\mathrm{an}} \quad\text{over }X_\Omega^{\mathrm{an}}.$$ The notation on the left denotes the inverse image of $U$ under the composite map to $X_\Omega^{\mathrm{an}}$.
  - Explanation: Roots on every inverse collar permit replacement of branch disks by trivial covers and an exterior analytic map.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-point-transfer)): In Proposition <a href="#prop:patching" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:patching">4</a>, suppose that the composite $V_\Omega\to X_\Omega$ is isomorphic, over $X_\Omega$, to the base change of a finite morphism $V\to X_{\bar k}$ from a smooth proper connected curve, and assume that $\xi\in U$. If $\bar k(V)$ admits a $K_0$-embedding into $H$, then $\bar k(C)$ admits a $K_0$-embedding into $H$.
  - Explanation: Evaluation at the fixed valued point transfers a function-field embedding through the exterior analytic map.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-horizontal-closure)): Fix a positive integer $n$. Among finite extensions of $K_0$, the condition that every ramification index at a closed point of $X_{\bar k}$ divide $n$ is preserved by taking subextensions, finite composita, and Galois closures. It is also preserved by normalized pullback along a finite morphism of smooth proper curves and by composition on top with a finite étale cover. Consequently every finite cover of $X_{\bar k}$ with indices dividing $\ell$ has a $K_0$-embedding of its function field into $L_\ell$.
  - Explanation: Horizontal ramification bounds survive the stated field operations, normalized pullback, and an étale upper cover.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-annular-unit)): An analytic unit on an open disk over $\Omega$ has constant absolute value in $\|\Omega^\times\|$. If $A=\{r<\|S\|<R\}$ is an open annulus and $u\in\mathcal O(A)^\times$, then $$u=cS^d(1+h),\qquad c\in\Omega^\times,\quad d\in\mathbb Z,\quad h\in\mathcal O(A),\quad \|h(x)\|<1\quad(x\in A),$$ where $cS^d$ is a term of the Laurent expansion of $u$. The function $1+h$ has an analytic $\ell$-th root on $A$. Consequently, on any analytic cover of $A$ on which $S$ has an $\ell$-th root, so does $u$.
  - Explanation: Annular units factor into a dominant monomial and a small unit, permitting extraction of prime-to-residue-characteristic roots.
- **C9** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-stable-edge-root)): Let $C/\Omega$ be a smooth proper connected curve of genus at least two, equipped with its stable skeleton. Let $A$ be a node annulus and $S$ an annular coordinate on $A$. There is a connected finite étale cover $C'\to C$ such that $S$ has an analytic $\ell$-th root on the entire inverse image of $A$. A single such cover can be chosen for any finite collection of node annuli.
  - Explanation: Stable node coordinates acquire roots on entire inverse annuli of a connected finite étale cover.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-etale-collars)): Let $C/\bar k$ be a smooth proper connected curve of genus at least two. Let $b_1,\ldots,b_s\in C(\bar k)$ be distinct, and let $B_i$ be pairwise disjoint sufficiently small coordinate disks in $C_\Omega^{\mathrm{an}}$ about $b_i$, with coordinates $t_i$ defined over $\bar k$ and vanishing simply at $b_i$. There is a connected finite étale cover $Y\to C_\Omega$ and concentric open collars $$A_i'=\{r_i<\|t_i\|<R_i\}\subset B_i,\qquad r_i,R_i\in\|\Omega^\times\|,\quad 0<r_i<R_i,$$ such that $t_i$ has an analytic $\ell$-th root on the entire inverse image of $A_i'$ in $Y^{\mathrm{an}}$.
  - Explanation: Resolution of nonsingularities moves small disk collars into stable node annuli where coordinate roots are available.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-first-stage)): The field $L_\ell$ is contained in $H$.
  - Explanation: The intermediate horizontal-ramification compositum lies in the fixed completion.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-division-roots)): Let $J$ be an abelian variety over $\Omega$ with analytic uniformization $q:\mathbb T^{\rm an}\to J^{\rm an}$, where $\mathbb T$ is a split torus and the kernel $\Lambda\subset\mathbb T(\Omega)$ is a free lattice acting by translation. Suppose $q$ is a topological covering and a local analytic isomorphism. Let $A$ be an analytic space, let $a:A\to\mathbb T^{\rm an}$ be analytic, and let $m\ge1$ be an integer. Set $$B=A\times_{q\circ a,J^{\rm an},[m]}J^{\rm an}.$$ For every character $\chi$ of $\mathbb T$, the pullback of $\chi\circ a$ to $B$ has an analytic $m$-th root.
  - Explanation: Division on the uniformized abelian variety supplies character roots on every inverse sheet.
- **C13** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-arbitrary-roots)): Let $m\ge1$ be an integer, choose an integer $$D>v_p(m)+\frac1{p-1},$$ and let $\alpha\in\overline{k}^{\times}$ satisfy $a=v(\alpha)>2D$. There is a smooth, proper, connected curve $Q/\overline{k}$ and a finite map $Q\to\mathbb P^1_{\overline{k}}$ whose ramification indices divide $\ell$, such that the pullback of the coordinate $T$ has an analytic $m$-th root on the entire inverse image, over $\Omega$, of $$A^\circ=\{D<v(T)<a-D\}.$$
  - Explanation: Auxiliary covers supply coordinate roots of arbitrary positive order while retaining the horizontal ramification bound.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-decomposition)): Let $k/\mathbb Q_p$ be finite and let $X/k$ be a smooth proper geometrically connected curve of genus at least two. Put $K=k(X)$ and let $\widetilde K\subset K^{\mathrm{sep}}$ be the extension corresponding to the full arithmetic étale fundamental group of $X$. Let $w$ be a real-valued rank-one valuation of $K$ extending the $p$-adic valuation, normalized by $w(p)=1$, and fix a prolongation $w^{\mathrm{sep}}$ to $K^{\mathrm{sep}}$. Restriction induces an isomorphism of profinite groups $$D_{w^{\mathrm{sep}}}(K^{\mathrm{sep}}/K) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } D_{\widetilde w}(\widetilde K/K), \qquad \widetilde w=w^{\mathrm{sep}}\|_{\widetilde K}.$$
  - Explanation: Restriction identifies the absolute and étale decomposition groups at a fixed rank-one prolongation.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-no-rank-one-section)): With $k$, $X$, $K$, and $\widetilde K$ as in Corollary <a href="#cor:decomposition" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:decomposition">14</a>, let $w$ be any rank-one valuation of $K$ extending the $p$-adic valuation and let $\widetilde w$ be any prolongation to $\widetilde K$. The projection $$D_{\widetilde w}(\widetilde K/K)\longrightarrow G_k$$ has no continuous group-homomorphic section.
  - Explanation: A rank-one decomposition group extending the p-adic valuation cannot support a section onto the constant-field Galois group.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-localization-image)): Let $C/K$ be a smooth proper geometrically connected curve of genus at least two over a number field. Then $$\label{eq:localization-image} \operatorname{loc}\bigl(\operatorname{Sec}(C/K)\bigr) =C(\mathbb A_K)_\bullet^{\mathrm{f\text{-}cov}}.$$
  - Explanation: The localization image of global sections equals the modified finite-cover descent locus.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-global-descent)): Let $C/K$ be a smooth proper geometrically connected curve of genus at least two over a number field. If $$\label{eq:finite-cover-equality} C(\mathbb A_K)_\bullet^{\mathrm{f\text{-}cov}}=C(K),$$ then $\kappa_C:C(K)\to\operatorname{Sec}(C/K)$ is bijective.
  - Explanation: Equality of the finite-cover descent locus with rational points implies bijectivity of the global point-section map.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-global-abelian-target)): Let $C/K$ be a smooth proper geometrically connected curve of genus at least two over a number field. The map $\kappa_C$ is bijective if either of the following conditions holds:* 1. *there is a nonconstant $K$-morphism $C\to A$ to an abelian variety with $A(K)$ finite and $\operatorname{Sha}(A/K)_{\mathrm{div}}=0$;* 2. *the Jacobian $J$ of $C$ has $J(K)$ finite and $\operatorname{Sha}(J/K)_{\mathrm{div}}=0$.
  - Explanation: A finite rational-point abelian target with trivial divisible Tate–Shafarevich subgroup, or the analogous Jacobian condition, gives the global bijection.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-global-selmer-target)): Let $C/\mathbb Q$ be a smooth proper geometrically connected curve of genus at least two. Suppose that there is a nonconstant $\mathbb Q$-morphism $C\to E$ to an elliptic curve and that $s_p(E)=0$ for at least one prime $p$. Then $\kappa_C$ is bijective.
  - Explanation: A nonconstant elliptic target over the rationals with vanishing Selmer corank gives the global bijection.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-global-modular-curves)): For every positive integer $N$ such that the smooth projective geometrically connected curve $X_0(N)/\mathbb Q$ has genus at least two, the map $\kappa_{X_0(N)}$ is bijective. The same assertion holds for the smooth projective geometrically connected curve $X_1(N)/\mathbb Q$ whenever its genus is at least two.
  - Explanation: The point-section maps for the stated smooth projective modular curves are bijective under the genus hypothesis.

          "exact_quote": "Let $p$ be a prime, let $k/\\mathbb Q_p$ be finite, and let $X/k$ be a smooth proper geometrically connected curve of genus at least two.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-normal-form",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:C\\to X_{\\bar k}$ be a finite surjective morphism of smooth proper connected curves, and let $b\\in X(\\bar k)$. Fix a rational function $t_b$ which is a local parameter at $b$. There is an open disk $B_b$ about $b$ in $X_\\Omega^{\\mathrm{an}}$, with coordinate $t_b$, such that its full inverse image in $C_\\Omega^{\\mathrm{an}}$ is a disjoint union of open disks. On the disk about $c\\in f^{-1}(b)$ there is a coordinate $z_c$ for which $$t_b=z_c^{e_c},$$ where $e_c$ is the ramification index at $c$. No condition that $e_c$ be prime to $p$ is required. For finitely many distinct points $b$, the disks can be chosen disjoint and disjoint from the fixed point $\\xi$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-patching",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:C\\to X_{\\bar k}$ be as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:local-normal-form\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:local-normal-form\">3</a>, of degree $d$, with branch set $B$. Choose pairwise disjoint disks $B_b$ as in that lemma. For each $b\\in B$, let $m_b$ be a positive multiple of all ramification indices above $b$. Suppose that $g:Y\\to X_\\Omega$ is a finite surjective morphism from a smooth proper connected curve and that there are radii $0<r_b<R_b$ in $|\\Omega^\\times|$, strictly inside $B_b$, such that $g^*t_b$ has an analytic $m_b$-th root on the whole inverse image of $$A_b=\\{r_b<|t_b|<R_b\\}.$$ Set $$U=X_\\Omega^{\\mathrm{an}}\\setminus\n       \\bigcup_{b\\in B}\\{|t_b|\\le r_b\\},$$ where each indicated closed disk is taken inside $B_b$.       "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-horizontal-closure",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a positive integer $n$. Consequently every finite cover of $X_{\\bar k}$ with indices dividing $\\ell$ has a $K_0$-embedding of its function field into $L_\\ell$.",
          "source_id": "PM-019.2"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **geometric section** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A section whose class comes from a rational point is called *geometric*.
- **universal étale function field** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Let $\widetilde K\subset K^{\mathrm{sep}}$ be the compositum of the function fields of all connected finite étale covers of $X$, with all their $K$-embeddings in $K^{\mathrm{sep}}$.
- **real-valued rank-one norm** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A real-valued rank-one norm on $K$ means a nonarchimedean field absolute value with values in $\mathbb R_{\ge0}$; in particular its support is zero.
- **horizontal ramification indices** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): We call the ramification indices of this map at closed points *horizontal ramification indices*.
- **horizontal-ramification compositum** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): Inside $K^{\mathrm{sep}}$, let $L_\ell$ be the compositum of all finite Galois extensions $F/K_0$ whose horizontal ramification indices divide $\ell$.
- **modified adelic set** ([source](#eq:descent-inclusions)): For a proper $K$-scheme $V$, use the modified adelic set $$V(\mathbb A_K)_\bullet =\prod_{v\nmid\infty}V(K_v) \times\prod_{v\ \mathrm{real}}\pi_0\bigl(V(K_v)\bigr),$$ where $\pi_0$ denotes the set of connected components.
- **Tate–Shafarevich group** ([source](#subsec:global-criteria)): For an abelian variety $A/K$, let $$\operatorname{Sha}(A/K) =\ker\left(H^1(K,A)\longrightarrow\prod_v H^1(K_v,A)\right),$$ and write $\operatorname{Sha}(A/K)_{\mathrm{div}}$ for its maximal divisible subgroup.
- **linear descent set** ([source](#subsec:global-restrictions)): Write $D_{\mathrm{lin}}(C)$ for the ordinary adelic descent set: $P$ belongs to it if, for every $C$-torsor $T\to C$ under a linear algebraic $K$-group $G$, it lifts to $T^\xi(\mathbb A_K)$ for some global twist $\xi\in H^1(K,G)$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-patching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-patching): Trivialize full inverse collars and glue finite étale analytic algebras. -> An étale cover with an exterior analytic map
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-first-stage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-first-stage): Resolve collars into stable-node annuli, then patch and evaluate. -> Containment of the horizontal-ramification compositum
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-arbitrary-roots) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-arbitrary-roots): Use parallel skeleton edges, toric characters, Jacobian division and a trimmed collar. -> Arbitrary coordinate roots with bounded horizontal ramification
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-thm-completion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-arbitrary-roots-2): Pull back auxiliary covers, patch, transfer embeddings, and identify normal images. -> The specified separable closure lies in the fixed completion
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-cor-decomposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-decomposition): Extend valuation-preserving automorphisms isometrically and use density. -> Isomorphism of the decomposition groups
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-rank-one-section) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-rank-one-section): Birational localization and approximation for inequivalent henselian valuations. -> Exclusion of rank-one section support
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-section) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-rank-one-section-2): Classify localized valuations and retain horizontal refinements. -> Surjectivity of the local point-section map
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-thm-section) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-rank-one-section-3): Kummer torsors and the local logarithm eliminate divisible Jacobian classes. -> Injectivity of the local point-section map
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-prop-localization-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-localization-image): Apply the finite-torsor descent bridge in both directions. -> Localization equals the finite-cover descent locus
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-global-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-global-descent): Combine descent equality with finite support and local injectivity. -> Global point-section bijection

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **horizontal_pullback**: Normalized horizontal pullback replaces the ramification index by $e/\gcd(e,r)$, which divides $e$.
- **whole_inverse_collar**: Patching requires an analytic root on the whole inverse image of each branch collar.
- **specified_completion**: The completion containment concerns the specified separable closure in the fixed ambient completion.
- **middle_collar**: The middle collar is subject to the strict logarithm and exponential convergence threshold and the strict width condition.
- **global_descent**: The global section criterion retains equality of the finite-cover descent locus with rational points as a hypothesis.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **single_sheet**: Patch after finding a root on a selected sheet. -> The remaining sheets need not trivialize.
- **reduction**: Use the horizontal ramification bound as a tame-reduction certificate. -> The bound concerns closed-point valuations trivial on constants.
- **ramified_tower**: Compose arbitrarily ramified covers while retaining the same horizontal bound. -> The upper cover can introduce additional ramification.
- **abstract_embedding**: Replace the specified field containment by an arbitrary abstract embedding. -> An abstract embedding need not identify the chosen subfield in the ambient completion.
- **unconditional_global**: Deduce the global section conjecture solely from local point-theoreticity. -> The arithmetic descent equality remains unestablished.
- **effective_bound**: Turn local finiteness of localization into an effective uniform enumeration of global sections. -> The localization restriction does not provide such an enumeration.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The horizontal pullback formula is a finite exact index calculation. The computation does not decide the analytic existence or section-conjecture assertions.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of triples [n,e,r], at most 4096 triples.
# PRECONDITIONS: Integers (not booleans) from 1 through 16; e divides n.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of normalized indices; INVALID for any inadmissible input.
# TIME: At most 4096 exact gcd computations on bounded integers.
# SPACE: Linear in the admitted list length.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact python-flint fmpz gcd and integer division.
# FAILURE: Malformed or inadmissible input yields INVALID with successful process exit.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz

def calculate(payload):
    if type(payload) is not list or len(payload) > 4096:
        return "INVALID"
    result = []
    for row in payload:
        if type(row) is not list or len(row) != 3:
            return "INVALID"
        if any(type(x) is not int or x < 1 or x > 16 for x in row):
            return "INVALID"
        n, e, r = row
        if n % e != 0:
            return "INVALID"
        divisor = int(fmpz(e).gcd(fmpz(r)))
        result.append(e // divisor)
    return result

try:
    payload = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
    answer = calculate(payload)
except (ValueError, TypeError):
    answer = "INVALID"
print(json.dumps(answer, separators=(",", ":")))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source proves existence and identification results for curves and sections; an executable software specification is ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The graph is a skeleton of a nonarchimedean curve; a game or simulation model and its dynamics are ABSENT. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source constructs algebraic covers and analytic roots; an end-to-end computational tool contract is ABSENT. |
| algorithms | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The local pullback proof supplies an exact integer calculation for a horizontal ramification index. This transfers to checking index data when the geometric hypotheses are already established. |
| data_structures | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The construction explicitly tracks a graph with loops, chords, edge lengths, expansion factors and marked residue curves. These data motivate a structured representation of a metrized complex; they do not specify a complete computational representation of the algebraic curves. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source treats valued fields and curve covers; hardware operations and accelerator requirements are ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The section map identifies rational points under geometric hypotheses; a cryptographic primitive, threat model and security reduction are ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source concerns arithmetic sections and descent; learning objectives, training data and statistical guarantees are ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The collar choices establish existence of analytic roots; an optimisation objective and an operational feasible-set contract are ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Gluing analytic covers and taking field composita are mathematical operations; a distributed protocol and its failure model are ABSENT. |
| other (arithmetic and nonarchimedean geometry) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The paper identifies rational points with full arithmetic étale section classes over p-adic fields and derives global consequences subject to finite-cover descent hypotheses. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): The local bijection requires a smooth proper geometrically connected curve of genus at least two over a finite extension of the stated local field. Blocked use: ['Extending the result to nonproper, singular, geometrically disconnected, or lower-genus curves.']
- **L02** (formal_scope): The completion containment concerns specified field copies inside the same completion for a fixed prolonged norm. Blocked use: ['Replacing the specified containment by an unrelated abstract embedding.']
- **L03** (hypothesis): Patching requires coordinate roots on the whole inverse image of each boundary annulus. Blocked use: ['Using a root on a selected sheet to trivialize the full branched cover.']
- **L04** (formal_scope): Point transfer retains the valued point and its residue-field embedding; the exterior analytic map is not asserted to descend. Blocked use: ['Assuming that the analytic map or its local trivialization descends to the algebraic constant field.']
- **L05** (counterexample_scope): The horizontal ramification closure lemma does not concern tame reduction in residue characteristic matching the local prime and requires an étale upper cover for composition. Blocked use: ['Composing arbitrary ramified covers while retaining the horizontal index bound.', 'Transferring horizontal ramification conclusions to reduction ramification.']
- **L06** (hypothesis): The arbitrary-root construction requires a sufficiently long annulus and a strict logarithm–exponential convergence margin. Blocked use: ['Applying the middle-collar construction at equality in the thickness or width bounds.']
- **L07** (edge_case): Horizontal refinements may lie over a non-rational closed point; rationality is forced by surjection onto the base Galois group. Blocked use: ['Equating every horizontal refinement with a rational-point section without the surjection hypothesis.']
- **L08** (hypothesis): The general global criterion requires the finite-cover descent locus to equal the diagonal rational points. Blocked use: ['Deducing the global section conjecture for arbitrary number-field curves from the local theorem alone.']
- **L09** (formal_scope): Modified adelic tuples retain connected components at real places and omit complex places. Blocked use: ['Treating a modified localization tuple as a unique ordinary adelic tuple.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The Selmer-corank specialization requires a nonconstant rational morphism to an elliptic curve and vanishing corank at some prime. Blocked use: ['Applying the specialization without the elliptic target or the corank hypothesis.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): Local finiteness of the localization image requires the absence of a CM subfield and supplies no effective or uniform bound. Blocked use: ['Inferring injectivity of localization, finiteness of the global section set, or an effective uniform bound.']
- **L12** (nonconstructive): Existence of analytic roots on covers is not a finite computational procedure for recovering all rational points or section classes. Blocked use: ['Treating finite arithmetic checks of ramification indices as certificates of the analytic or section-conjecture theorems.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the local <i>p</i>-adic section conjecture for smooth proper geometrically connected curves of genus at least two, over every finite extension of ℚ<sub><i>p</i></sub>. Combined with established finite-descent theorems, this also yields the global section conjecture for smooth proper geometrically connected curves of genus at least two over number fields when their finite-cover descent locus equals their rational points; this includes $`X_0(N)`$ and $`X_1(N)`$ of genus at least two over ℚ.
